# Implementation Plan

## Overview

This plan implements the `security-scan-remediation-high` design: a baseline test run at `4a3f960` (task 0), one task per remediation area (tasks 1 to 9), the owner-decision documentation (task 10), full verification against the baseline (task 11), the pinned-scanner rescan (task 12), the ledger follow-ups (task 13), the neutral-label swap (task 14) and the final checker and closing report (task 15). Tasks run in number order with one exception: task 9 (R15, the last part of Deploy A, whose checkpoint 9.12 snapshots Deploy A's files, tasks 1, 6, 7 and 9, outside the repository) runs before task 8 (R14 DynamoDB, Deploy B), because task 8 reuses 9.5's role helper, 9.6's post-fix record, 9.7's `regen_baselines.py` and the `4a3f960` synth that 9.9 keeps, and it changes files whose Deploy A state that snapshot must keep. The execution order is 0 to 7, 9, 8, then 10 to 15. This run makes no commit: the owner commits Deploy A from 9.12's snapshot, Deploy B from 8.10's path list and the remaining changes, all in the owner-gated section. Steps that need the owner's credentials, accounts, devices or approval are listed in the owner-gated section at the end and are not executed in this run.

Snapshot rule, from 8.1 on: 9.12's copy never runs again, because the shared files then also hold task 8's changes. A later change to a path that `$WS/deploy-a-snapshot/paths.txt` lists, such as a fix that task 11 or 12.8 sends back, the late 6.3 branch (owner-gated section 1) or a 14.2 rewording, goes into both trees: the live file and its copy in `$WS/deploy-a-snapshot/tree/`, as the same hunk in each for a path that task 8 also changes. A path that joins Deploy A, like the approvals file with a late 6.3, is added to `paths.txt`, and its copy starts from its committed content (`git -C "$REPO" show HEAD:<path>`). The path's line in `sha256.txt` is re-recorded from its snapshot copy and, if 8.10 has listed the path, its line in `$WS/deploy-b-sha256.txt` from the live file; a later change to Deploy B content alone, such as a fix to task 8's key or grants, stays out of the snapshot and re-records only that second line. When a Baseline_Template, the post-fix record or the approvals file changes, 9.10's review repeats over the snapshot copies and, once 8.9 has run, 8.9's over the live files. The affected gates run again. Owner-gated sections 2 and 3 check both hash lists, so a change made any other way stops the owner's commits.

**Deploys A and B are deployed, verified and on the trunk (2026-10-06). Owner-gated sections 1 to 4 are done; 5 to 9 remain. Resume here.**
- **Approval.** Sections 1 to 3 and the commits ran on the owner's approval, "ok ready for deploy and commits": Ryan Vanderwerf, 2026-10-05. This handoff was recorded on 2026-10-06.
- **Records.** `deploy/` below means `$REPO/../.agents/security-scan-d5f01064/deploy/`: `preflight.md` (section 1), `commits.md`, `deploy-a-result.md`, `verify-a-result.md`, `deploy-b-result.md` and `final-report.md`. Each owner-gated section 1 to 4 below has a `Result (2026-10-06)` line.
- **Deployed and pushed.**
  - `origin/integration/all-specs` = `3e49de6`: `d7bf0d6` (Deploy A, the cherry-pick of `acf2fdd`) and `3e49de6` (Deploy B, the cherry-pick of `aba57cb`), both fast-forwards on `527ce4e`, never forced.
  - Both are deployed to every EdgeCVPortal stack in the one portal account and region, with no failure or rollback. Each passed its 30-minute watch.
  - The worktree `.worktrees/remediation-deploy` is removed. The branch `remediation-deploy` was deleted after the merge below, because the trunk holds its commits.
- **Published (2026-10-06, owner decision 9 in requirements.md).** `remediation` contains:
  - `acf2fdd` (Deploy A), `aba57cb` (Deploy B), the spec commit and the owner-decision commit;
  - "Merge integration/all-specs into remediation", which brings in the trunk at `3e49de6`.

  It was pushed to `origin/remediation`, and `integration/all-specs` was fast-forwarded to it. Neither push was forced. Before the push, the merged tree differed from the deployed trunk only in this spec directory.
- **Still uncommitted.** The section 7 code group stays uncommitted in the working tree of the main clone, on `remediation`. It covers the device-side code under `src/`, the vendored catalog copy, `test/on-hardware/register_vllm_models.py` and the rest. Build steering commits on-device changes only after they are verified on real hardware.
- **Next, in order:**
  1. The owner checks from Verify A and Deploy B, listed below.
  2. Section 5: build and push the detector export image, then deploy its digest.
  3. Section 6: the LocalServer builds, one at a time, and the device checks on each architecture. The owner supplies the device SSH passwords.
  4. Section 7: the code commit, only AFTER section 6's device verification, with the devices named in its message. Its second commit, the spec directory, is already made, so a later spec change goes into a new `docs(spec)` commit.
  5. Section 8: the ComputeStack deploy that publishes the `WorkflowCoreLayer` catalog note.
  6. Section 9: the platform rescan and its `rescan.json` record.
  7. Done on 2026-10-06: the push and the merge into the trunk (Requirement 18.3, owner decision 9). Later commits go on `remediation`. Before pushing them, fetch and merge `origin/integration/all-specs`, then fast-forward the trunk to `remediation`, as in that first push. Never force.
- **Owner checks (Verify A section 10 and Deploy B section 9).** Each writes Portal data, publishes, starts a job, opens a tunnel or touches a device, so none ran here:
  - Alert delivery through the encrypted topic. The topic has 0 subscriptions, so either make one test publish (it can only show a `MessageId`) or, after the next real training or compilation failure alert, check `NumberOfMessagesPublished` ≥ 1 and `NumberOfNotificationsFailed` = 0 for that hour, and that neither events handler logged `Error sending SNS notification`.
  - One auto-labeling job end to end, through the `alias/aws/sqs` queue, watching both workers' Errors and the DLQ.
  - The redrive check on the auto-label pair, as in section 2's After.
  - Quick Setup of a new station in a same-account use case, with no `AccessDenied` (device phase).
  - An SSH tunnel from the portal to a same-account device (device phase).
  - The camera-shadow report and account-sync ack paths, passively. Their encryption didn't change.
  - Deploy B: an account sync to a test device, removing a test device, and creating a test account or setting a password, with no warning, no handler ERROR and no KMS error in CloudTrail. Nobody disables the account-tables key or schedules its deletion.
  - The JWT layer follow-up: `JwtLayer` is empty, so the hardened authorizer can't import on Lambda until `edge-cv-portal/backend/layers/jwt/build.sh` populates it. Run that before anyone attaches the authorizer to an API. No API uses it today: all 10 authorizers are Cognito.
- **Observations (pre-existing, not caused by A or B; the owner's call).**
  - Each deploy deletes the SageMaker enabler rule in cleanup, because the custom resource's physical id changes. The stack output still says "enabled successfully". No function depends on the rule.
  - `DeploymentsRole` gets about 96 `iot:DescribeJobExecution` AccessDenied an hour, at the same rate before and after the deploys.
  - `dda-portal-camera-shadow-reports-dlq` holds a backlog of about 4,700 messages that is ageing out.
  - Six API methods have `authorizationType NONE` by design: the four `/quick-setup/*` methods, `/auth/refresh` and `/shared-components/provision`.

## Task Dependency Graph

One task per wave, in execution order. No two tasks run in parallel: tasks 1 and 6 to 9 all edit `compute-stack.ts`, tasks 6 to 9 share the R14 and R15 Jest file, and 5.5 and 7.5 can each create `rescan.json`. The owner-gated section is not part of the graph.

```json
{
  "waves": [
    { "wave": 1, "tasks": ["0"], "description": "Baseline test run at 4a3f960, before any remediation task." },
    { "wave": 2, "tasks": ["1"], "description": "R6: harden the Portal JWT authorizer (Deploy A). Depends on wave 1." },
    { "wave": 3, "tasks": ["2"], "description": "R7: validate externally influenced subprocess arguments. Depends on wave 2." },
    { "wave": 4, "tasks": ["3"], "description": "R9: restrict URL fetching. Depends on wave 3." },
    { "wave": 5, "tasks": ["4"], "description": "R10: load legacy reference-image maps only from trusted files. Depends on wave 4." },
    { "wave": 6, "tasks": ["5"], "description": "R12: mark the camera-id SHA-1 hashes as non-security; 5.5 may create rescan.json. Depends on wave 5." },
    { "wave": 7, "tasks": ["6"], "description": "R14 SNS: encrypt the training-alerts topic (Deploy A). Depends on wave 6." },
    { "wave": 8, "tasks": ["7"], "description": "R14 SQS: encrypt the six queues (Deploy A); 7.5 may create rescan.json. Depends on wave 7." },
    { "wave": 9, "tasks": ["9"], "description": "R15: scope the IAM statements and refresh the Baseline_Templates (Deploy A); 9.12 snapshots Deploy A. Runs before task 8, which reuses its tools. Depends on wave 8." },
    { "wave": 10, "tasks": ["8"], "description": "R14 DynamoDB: one customer managed key for both account tables (Deploy B), after its start check on 9.12's snapshot. Depends on wave 9." },
    { "wave": 11, "tasks": ["10"], "description": "Document the accepted empty-prefix risk and the owner's decisions. Depends on wave 10." },
    { "wave": 12, "tasks": ["11"], "description": "Verify the finished change against the baseline on every lane, after tasks 1 to 10. Depends on wave 11." },
    { "wave": 13, "tasks": ["12"], "description": "Rescan with the pinned scanners and write the Rescan_Record. Depends on wave 12." },
    { "wave": 14, "tasks": ["13"], "description": "Ledger follow-ups after the rescan. Depends on wave 13." },
    { "wave": 15, "tasks": ["14"], "description": "Neutral-label swap, the last change before any push. Depends on wave 14." },
    { "wave": 16, "tasks": ["15"], "description": "Final checker and closing report. Depends on wave 15." }
  ]
}
```

    - Portal lane: the local `dda-portal-test:py311` image (Python 3.11.16, with pytest 9.0.3, moto 5.1.22, PyJWT 2.15.0 and hypothesis 6.168.1; the Portal Lambdas run Python 3.11 and 3.12), pinned by `docker image inspect dda-portal-test:py311 --format '{{.Id}}'` and used for both trees. In that image, run with `portal_py`'s `docker run` options below, `python3 -m venv --system-site-packages "$WS/portal-venv"` creates the one virtualenv for both trees; then, from `$REPO`, `"$WS/portal_py" -m pip install --no-cache-dir -r edge-cv-portal/backend/requirements-dev.txt PyJWT==2.15.0 numpy==2.4.6` completes it. PyJWT is the layer's pin, which task 1.4 adds to that file; numpy, which `test_export_checkpoint_static.py` imports and the image lacks, is the detector export image's pin (`edge-cv-portal/detector-export-image/requirements.lock`)
    - `$WS/portal_py`, a short `sh` script written here, runs that venv's Python in the pinned image with the given arguments: `t="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" || exit 2`, then `exec docker run --rm --pull=never --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)" -e HOME=/tmp -e PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 -v "$t":"$t" -v "$WS":"$WS" -w "$PWD" <Portal image id> "$WS/portal-venv/bin/python" "$@"`, with `$WS` written out. The tree a command runs in, `$REPO` or `$BASE`, is mounted at its own path, so every Portal command keeps its `cd` and paths, and the files a run writes stay owned by the host user. The gate lane stays on the host with the host's `python3`, because the synth gate skips inside containers
    - Container lane `py311`: `flask-app:latest` (python3.11), pinned by `docker image inspect flask-app:latest --format '{{.Id}}'`
    - Container lane `py310`, the Python 3.10 lane for JetPack 6 (Requirement 16.5): the local `dda/flask-app:1.0.46` image (Ubuntu 22.04, built 2026-08-03), whose `python3.10` has pip and the app dependencies, and as fallback the local `dda/flask-app:arm64-1.1.0` (Ubuntu 22.04, built 2026-09-25). Both carry a registry prefix; find each with `docker image ls --format '{{.Repository}}:{{.Tag}}' | grep -m1 '/dda/flask-app:1.0.46$'` (or `arm64-1.1.0`), so this plan never names the registry, and pin it by image id
    - Import check for each `py310` suite, on `$BASE`: 0.4's `py310` command with `--collect-only -q` in place of the run. A suite that 1.0.46 can't import because of the image, such as a newer dependency, runs in the fallback if it passes the same check there, and in that image for both trees; a suite neither image imports is reported to the owner. When this plan was revised, every existing `py310` suite collected under 1.0.46, and the fallback's `python3.10` had no pip, `numpy` or `yaml`, because its app dependencies are installed for 3.11. On-device JetPack 6 verification stays in the owner-gated section

An entry that doesn't start with `https://` is dropped and logged at ERROR, because its keys would be fetched without TLS

  - [x] 1.2 Rewrite `validate_jwt_token` so only the header is read before verification
    - Result: done. The grep prints nothing; the line-153 decode and its `# nosem` are gone. Beyond the design, `construct_rsa_key` calls `public_bytes()` in place of the nonexistent `serialize()` (task 1 Result)
    - Files: `edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/jwt_authorizer.py`
    - Change, in order (design `R6 change`): (1) raise `AuthorizationError` if `ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` or `TRUSTED_ISSUERS` is empty; (2) read the header with `jwt.get_unverified_header(token)`, require `kid` to be a non-empty string, and use it only to select a key; delete the unverified payload decode on line 153, so no code derives `iss` from an unverified token, and reword the comment above it (line 147) to say only the header's `kid` is read
    - (3) Walk `TRUSTED_ISSUERS` in order: fetch each JWKS through the cached `get_jwks_keys`, skip an issuer whose fetch fails, and when the `kid` is found call `jwt.decode(token, construct_rsa_key(jwks_key), algorithms=["RS256"], audience=list(ALLOWED_AUDIENCES), issuer=issuer, options={"require": ["exp", "iss", "aud", "sub"]})`, where `issuer` is the configured issuer that owns the key, never the token's `iss`. On `jwt.InvalidSignatureError` continue to the next issuer holding the same `kid`; any other `jwt.InvalidTokenError` ends the walk with a denial
    - (4) If no token verified: raise `AuthorizationError("Key not found in any trusted JWKS: <kid>")` when no trusted JWKS holds the `kid`, otherwise re-raise the last `jwt.InvalidSignatureError` (mapped to `Invalid token signature`); (5) for the Cognito issuer require `claims.get("token_use") == "id"`, else raise `AuthorizationError("Token is not an ID token")`; `ISSUER_WHITELIST` issuers are checked by `aud` alone; (6) return the claims
    - The exception mapping (lines 201-213) stays, plus `jwt.MissingRequiredClaimError` mapped to `Token missing required claim: <name>`. Steps 1-6 run inside the existing `try`, so the kept `except Exception` re-raises their own `AuthorizationError`s as `Token validation failed: <reason>`; don't add `except AuthorizationError: raise`. `handler`, `extract_token_from_event`, `generate_policy` and `_safe_event_metadata` don't change
    - Tests: 1.5 and 1.6
    - Verify: `grep -n 'verify_signature' edge-cv-portal/backend/functions/jwt_authorizer.py` prints nothing, then the 1.5 run
    - _Requirements: 6.1, 6.2, 6.3, 6.4_

    - Result: done; 24 passed, 0 skipped. `importorskip(..., minversion=...)` breaks collection under this conftest's `sys.path`, where `backend/functions/packaging.py` shadows `packaging`. So the file imports PyJWT and skips below 2.8 itself (design change log)
    - Files: new `edge-cv-portal/backend/tests/test_jwt_authorizer_verification.py`
    - Setup: unit tests through `handler()` that check the returned policy. An RSA key pair is generated at test time with `cryptography`, tokens are signed with real RS256, and `get_jwks_keys` is monkeypatched to serve the public JWK, with no network access. The module is reloaded with each test's environment, as `_load_jwt_authorizer` does in the preservation suite, and the file starts with `pytest.importorskip("jwt", minversion="2.8")`
    - No key or token literal is committed; values are visibly fake, and no variable, attribute or keyword argument is named with `token`, `secret` or `password` (Requirement 5.3)
    - Accepted: a valid Cognito ID token, whose policy context equals the one built from its claims; a valid token from a whitelisted `https://` issuer with an allowed `aud`
    - Denied: a flipped signature byte; a payload whose `custom:role` changed after signing; an expired token; a token with no `exp`; an `iss` naming another pool; an `https://` issuer containing the configured pool path as a substring; a whitelisted `iss` on a token whose `kid` exists only in the pool's JWKS; a wrong `aud`; no `aud`
    - Key-id collision: two trusted issuers serve the same `kid`; a token signed with the second issuer's key is accepted, and one whose signature fails under both keys is denied with a WARNING log naming `Invalid token signature`
    - Also denied: a Cognito access token whose `client_id` is allowed; an unknown `kid`, with `Key not found in any trusted JWKS` in the WARNING log; `alg: none`; HS256; an empty `ALLOWED_AUDIENCES`; an `http://` `ISSUER_WHITELIST` entry, which must be ignored
    - Static: an AST check that the module has no `jwt.decode` call with `verify_signature` set to `False`
    - Tests: the cases above, all new in this file
    - Verify: `cd "$REPO/edge-cv-portal" && "$WS/portal_py" -m pytest backend/tests/test_jwt_authorizer_verification.py -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` passes with zero skips (some hosts carry PyJWT 1.x in system site-packages)
    - _Requirements: 6.1, 6.2, 6.3, 6.4, 6.5, 5.3_

  - [x] 3.1 Fetch payload references through a gated opener with per-hop redirect checks
    - Result: done. `_build_opener(allowed_prefixes, context)` holds only `ProxyHandler`, `UnknownHandler`, `HTTPHandler`, `HTTPSHandler(context=...)`, `HTTPDefaultErrorHandler`, `_GatedRedirectHandler` and `HTTPErrorProcessor`; `_fetch_http(source, allowed_prefixes)` passes it the context it chose at `4a3f960` (design change log, row 1). `http_error_302` refuses a scheme other than empty or `http(s)` before the standard method runs, and 301, 303, 307 and, where defined, 308 are bound to it; `redirect_request` checks scheme, downgrade, userinfo and prefixes and closes the response on refusal. The grep prints nothing (rc 1), then the 3.3 run passes
    - Files: `src/backend/workflow_engine/payload_fetch.py`
    - Change: `_build_opener(allowed_prefixes)` returns an `OpenerDirector` with only `ProxyHandler()` (so `http_proxy` and `https_proxy` keep working), `UnknownHandler`, `HTTPHandler`, `HTTPSHandler(context=https_ssl_context())`, `HTTPDefaultErrorHandler`, `_GatedRedirectHandler(allowed_prefixes)` and `HTTPErrorProcessor`; there is no FTP, file or data handler. `_fetch_http(source, allowed_prefixes)` calls `_build_opener(allowed_prefixes).open(source, timeout=REFERENCE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SEC)`, with its status and size logic unchanged
    - The dispatcher's `http(s)` call (line 245, `data = _fetch_http(value)`) passes the new argument: `_fetch_http(value, allowed_prefixes)`. The dispatcher's routing doesn't otherwise change
    - `_GatedRedirectHandler` subclasses `urllib.request.HTTPRedirectHandler`. Its `http_error_302` checks the scheme first, reading the target as the standard library does (the first `Location` header, else `URI`): a scheme, parsed with `urlsplit` and lowercased, that is neither empty nor `http` or `https` is refused, before the standard method can raise its own `HTTPError` with the raw target. The class binds `http_error_301`, `http_error_303` and `http_error_307` to this override, and `http_error_308` where the base class defines it (CPython 3.11; 3.10 doesn't follow 308)
    - `redirect_request` then checks the absolute target: scheme `http` or `https` (case-insensitive), no `https` to `http` downgrade, no userinfo, and `_check_allowed(newurl, allowed_prefixes)` passes, with the first hop's rule that an empty list allows any remote source
    - On refusal, either method closes the redirect response and raises `PayloadReferenceError` naming the rejected scheme or the redacted destination; `_fetch_http` already re-raises it unchanged (lines 313-314). urllib's limits of 10 redirects and 4 repeats stay
    - Tests: 3.3 and 3.4
    - Verify: `git -C "$REPO" grep -n 'urlopen(' -- src/backend/workflow_engine/payload_fetch.py` prints nothing, then the 3.3 run
    - _Requirements: 9.1, 9.3_

The 3.3 run passes

    - `_redact(uri)` returns `scheme://host[:port]/path`: it drops userinfo and the fragment and replaces any query with `?<redacted>`; a URI that can't be parsed becomes `<unparseable URI>`.   - [x] 4.1 Add `open_trusted_legacy_map` to the `src/backend` copy
    - Result: done. `TRUSTED_LEGACY_MAP_ROOTS` (the two roots), `UntrustedLegacyMapError(ValueError)`, `_refusal`, `_below_trusted_root`, `_check_trusted_stat` and `open_trusted_legacy_map`, following steps 1 to 6; steps 2 and 4 compare with the roots as written, never resolved (design change log `Roots compared as written`, after task 4's review). The message names the path, the file it resolves to when that differs, one of `Outside the trusted roots`, `Not a regular file`, `Owned by uid N, not root or the effective uid M` or `Group- or world-writable (mode 0NNN)`, and the fix with both roots. No new line carries a `dill` token. The 4.4 run passes
    - Files: `src/backend/lyra_science_processing_utils/model_processors/reference_image_map_migration.py`
    - Change: add the module constant `TRUSTED_LEGACY_MAP_ROOTS` with exactly two entries, `/aws_dda/greengrass/v2/packages/artifacts-unarchived` (component artifacts, written only by the Greengrass nucleus) and `/aws_dda/dda_triton/triton_model_repo` (the Triton model repository, `src/backend/dda_triton/constants.py:44`). It stays local to the module, which mustn't import the backend's `utils.constants`, because `lyra_science_processing_utils` also ships in the sandbox and the Triton python backend
    - `open_trusted_legacy_map(path)`: (1) `resolved = os.path.realpath(path)`; (2) `resolved` must lie strictly below one of the roots, so a path outside them is refused before anything is opened; (3) `fd = os.open(resolved, os.O_RDONLY | os.O_NOFOLLOW | os.O_CLOEXEC | os.O_NONBLOCK)`, so a symlink swapped in after step 1 fails with `ELOOP` and a FIFO is refused by step 5 instead of blocking the open (`O_NONBLOCK` doesn't affect regular-file reads)
    - (4) The location check is repeated on the descriptor actually opened, not on a re-resolved path: `os.readlink(f"/proc/self/fd/{fd}")` must lie strictly below one of the roots, compared as in step 2; (5) `os.fstat(fd)` must show a regular file owned by uid 0 or the effective uid, with neither `S_IWGRP` nor `S_IWOTH` set; (6) return `os.fdopen(fd, "rb")`. On any refusal the descriptor is closed first
    - A refusal raises `UntrustedLegacyMapError`, a `ValueError` subclass, naming the path and the failed condition (outside the roots; not a regular file; `Owned by uid N`; group- or world-writable), with the fix hint: move the file under a model artifact root, or make it root-owned and not group- or world-writable (`chown root`, `chmod go-w`). Nothing is loaded or written
    - New code lines carry no `dill` token outside comments and docstrings, because `test/backend-test/security/repo_audit.py` allows it only on lines with a documented `# nosem`
    - Tests: 4.4 and 4.5
    - Verify: the 4.4 run
    - _Requirements: 10.1, 10.2_

A hit in a commit message or a committed diff is reported to the owner before any push, because removing it means rewriting local history, which needs the owner's approval

## Owner-gated (not executed in this run)

Nothing in this section runs in this run, and none of it is a task item. Each step needs the owner's AWS credentials, portal or use-case accounts, lab devices (SSH to them needs the owner's passwords) or an approval this run can't give. The order follows design `Deploy order`, `Device and image rollout` and `Device and account verification`. Commands use `REPO`, `INFRA=$REPO/edge-cv-portal/infrastructure`, `SPEC=$REPO/.kiro/specs/security-scan-remediation-high` and `WS`. This run makes no commit. The owner makes the Deploy A commit at the start of section 2, from 9.12's snapshot, and the Deploy B commit at the start of section 3, from 8.10's path list; their hashes are written `<deploy-a>` and `<deploy-b>` here.

### 1. Read-only account checks, before any deploy

- Where: each portal account and region, starting with a development account
- Preconditions: `aws sts get-caller-identity` succeeds for the account, and `AWS_REGION` names its region
- The 6.1 publisher checks for every account and region that `$WS/r14-sns-publisher-check.json` marks `owner-gated`: `aws sns list-topics` (the topic ARN ends in `:dda-portal-training-alerts`), `aws cloudwatch describe-alarms --alarm-types MetricAlarm CompositeAlarm`, `aws events list-rule-names-by-target --target-arn <topic ARN>` once per bus that `aws events list-event-buses` returns, and `aws sns get-topic-attributes --topic-arn <topic ARN>`. If any finds an AWS service publisher, stop before section 2's Deploy A commit: the topic takes task 6.3's customer managed key and edit O1 under the Overview's snapshot rule: in the working tree and, the same way, in Deploy A's snapshot copies (9.12), with the approvals file added to `paths.txt`, through 9.10's and 8.9's reviews; then 8.10 runs again
- The six queues' current encryption: `for q in dda-portal-camera-shadow-reports dda-portal-camera-shadow-reports-dlq dda-portal-account-sync-acks dda-portal-account-sync-acks-dlq dda-portal-autolabel-queue dda-portal-autolabel-queue-dlq; do aws sqs get-queue-attributes --queue-url "$(aws sqs get-queue-url --queue-name "$q" --query QueueUrl --output text)" --attribute-names All; done`
- The CloudFormation execution role's KMS access, needed for Deploy B: `aws iam list-attached-role-policies --role-name "cdk-hnb659fds-cfn-exec-role-<account>-$AWS_REGION"` shows `AdministratorAccess`, CDK's default bootstrap, or `aws iam simulate-principal-policy --policy-source-arn "arn:aws:iam::<account>:role/cdk-hnb659fds-cfn-exec-role-<account>-$AWS_REGION" --action-names kms:CreateGrant kms:DescribeKey` allows both
- The synthesized template size and the nested-stack resource counts, from 11.6's `$WS/head-final/deploy-constraints.json`

One portal account and region (us-east-1); the stack is in no other region. The 6.1 publisher re-check found no AWS service publisher (branch 6.2, so 6.3 and O1 aren't needed) and 0 subscriptions. All six queues were SSE-SQS with no key. The CloudFormation execution role has `AdministratorAccess`, and `kms:CreateGrant` and `kms:DescribeKey` simulate as allowed. ComputeStack at head-final is 658,503 bytes and 251 resources; the 8 nested-stack counts equal `4a3f960`. Record: `$REPO/../.agents/security-scan-d5f01064/deploy/preflight.md`

### 2. Deploy A: EdgeCVPortalComputeStack with R6, R14 SNS, R14 SQS and R15 sites 1 to 3

- Preconditions: section 1 done for this account; no station's Quick Setup is running, because site 1 changes the role that issues setup credentials and a call can be refused briefly while policy documents swap; no LocalServer component build is running (build steering)
- Commit Deploy A, once, before the first account's deploy (design `IAM approvals-file updates`, step 4). Check both hash lists first: `cd "$REPO" && sha256sum -c --quiet "$WS/deploy-a-snapshot/sha256.txt"` may report only paths listed in `$WS/deploy-b-paths.txt`, and `cd "$REPO" && sha256sum -c --quiet "$WS/deploy-b-sha256.txt"` passes. Any other difference is a change made outside the Overview's snapshot rule: stop and apply the rule to it first. Build the commit from the snapshot in a temporary worktree at the branch's current commit: `git -C "$REPO" worktree add --detach "$WS/commit-a" HEAD`, `tar -C "$WS/deploy-a-snapshot/tree" -cf - . | tar -C "$WS/commit-a" -xf -`, then `cd "$WS/commit-a" && git add --pathspec-from-file="$WS/deploy-a-snapshot/paths.txt" && git commit -m "fix(security): Deploy A, R6 authorizer, R14 topic and queue encryption, R15 IAM scoping"`. Then `git -C "$REPO" merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD "$(git -C "$WS/commit-a" rev-parse HEAD)"` must exit 0, so the reset only moves the branch forward and drops no commit; otherwise stop, because `remediation` moved after the worktree was created. After it, `git -C "$REPO" reset --mixed "$(git -C "$WS/commit-a" rev-parse HEAD)"` moves `remediation` and the index to that commit, `<deploy-a>`, and leaves every working-tree file as it is (`git merge --ff-only` would refuse, because the same files have local changes), and `git -C "$REPO" worktree remove "$WS/commit-a"` follows. Afterwards `git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain` lists no Deploy A path except the five in `$WS/deploy-b-paths.txt`
- Deploy from a temporary worktree at the Deploy A commit, so the main tree's uncommitted work stays untouched: `git -C "$REPO" worktree add --detach "$WS/deploy-a" <deploy-a>`, then `cd "$WS/deploy-a/edge-cv-portal/infrastructure" && npm ci && npm run build`
- Context: the credentials and the `CDK_DEFAULT_ACCOUNT`, `CDK_DEFAULT_REGION` and `AWS_REGION` exports that `edge-cv-portal/deploy-frontend.sh` sets for its ComputeStack step, and its four context values, as in 11.7: `-c cloudFrontDomain=<FrontendStack output DistributionDomainName> -c trustedUseCaseAccountIds=<TRUSTED_USECASE_ACCOUNT_IDS, or the account id> -c dataBucketAllowlist=<DATA_BUCKET_ALLOWLIST>`, plus `-c portalRegistryEnforced=<value>` when `portal_registry_enforced_for_deploy` prints one. Called `<context>` below
- Review, the human gate, because the deploy doesn't prompt (design `In-place updates`): `npx cdk diff EdgeCVPortalComputeStack <context>`. It must show no resource replaced or destroyed, except a Lambda layer version whose asset content changed (11.7); no new resource, or only the topic key and its alias if 6.3 was taken; IAM changes that are exactly the R15 site 1 to 3 rescopes, plus O1 with 6.3, judged per role at grant level as the synth gate does, because ComputeStack minimizes its policies and the diff also shows statements that only change carrier or re-render merged. Expected with no grant change: DevicesRole's base `s3:GetObject` and `s3:PutObject` statement on `arn:aws:s3:::*/*` moves from `DevicesRoleOverflowPolicy11505496F` into `DevicesRoleDefaultPolicyA4BE7868`, the new `tunnel/*` statement lands in the overflow policy, and the merged `'*'` statements re-render with unchanged actions (four `logs:` read actions, `iot:DescribeEndpoint`, `s3:ListAllMyBuckets`, `sagemaker:ListWorkteams` and `tag:GetResources` on DevicesRole; `sts:GetCallerIdentity` on StationProvisioningRole); and Lambda changes to code and environment only, the environment change being `ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` on `JwtAuthorizerHandler`. Any other environment change means a context value differs from the deployed one: fix the value before deploying
- Deploy: `npx cdk deploy EdgeCVPortalComputeStack --require-approval never <context>`, then `git -C "$REPO" worktree remove "$WS/deploy-a"`
- After (design `R14 SQS tests` and `R15 tests`): the six queues' `KmsMasterKeyId` and `SqsManagedSseEnabled` (`alias/aws/sqs` on the auto-label pair, `true` on the other four); the topic's `KmsMasterKeyId` from `aws sns get-topic-attributes`, and that an alert still gets through the encrypted topic (design `R14 SNS preserving function`), because a publish that KMS refuses only leaves the handlers' ERROR line `Error sending SNS notification` (design `R14 SNS errors`): either `aws sns publish --topic-arn <topic ARN> --subject "DDA Portal test alert" --message "Test after Deploy A, no action needed"` returns a `MessageId` and the message reaches an existing subscription, or, where a test message must not reach subscribers, after the next training or compilation failure alert the topic's `NumberOfMessagesPublished` sum for that hour is at least 1 and its `NumberOfNotificationsFailed` sum is 0 (`aws cloudwatch get-metric-statistics --namespace AWS/SNS --metric-name <metric> --dimensions Name=TopicName,Value=dda-portal-training-alerts --statistics Sum --period 3600 --start-time <start> --end-time <end>`), and the log groups of the functions whose names contain `TrainingEventsHandler` and `CompilationEventsHandler` hold no `Error sending SNS notification` line since the deploy; one auto-labeling job end to end; the redrive check on the auto-label pair (disable `DdaAutolabelWorker`'s event source mapping, send one test message, receive it with `--visibility-timeout 0` until SQS moves it to `dda-portal-autolabel-queue-dlq`, delete it from the DLQ and re-enable the mapping); Quick Setup of a new station in a same-account use case, in another region if possible, with no `AccessDenied` in the setup log; an SSH tunnel from the portal to a same-account device; and the `SageMakerEventBridgeStatus` output, `aws cloudformation describe-stacks --stack-name EdgeCVPortalComputeStack --query "Stacks[0].Outputs[?OutputKey=='SageMakerEventBridgeStatus'].OutputValue" --output text`, showing that the enabler succeeded
- Rollback (design `Rollback`): CloudFormation rolls back a failed update itself; a manual rollback reverts the commit and redeploys
- Result (2026-10-06): committed, pushed, deployed and verified. `<deploy-a>` = `acf2fdd` on `remediation` (local, 16 files, built from 9.12's snapshot; `commits.md`). Its cherry-pick `d7bf0d6` (parent `527ce4e`; the same changed lines, and the merged out-of-scope baseline also keeps the trunk's `app.py` line) was pushed to `origin/integration/all-specs` as a fast-forward at 04:38Z, then deployed with `edge-cv-portal/deploy-infrastructure.sh` from the temporary worktree `.worktrees/remediation-deploy`, under umask `0002`, with the deployed `cloudFrontDomain` and `portalRegistryEnforced=true` context, 04:42 to 04:59Z: 8 root stacks `✅`, no failure or rollback, and `QUICK_SETUP_BUNDLE_SHA256` unchanged. After: the auto-label pair is on `alias/aws/sqs` and the other four queues stay SSE-SQS; the topic is on `alias/aws/sns`; the three rescoped roles' live policies equal the deployed template and the post-fix record; `ALLOWED_AUDIENCES` is the portal client id; unauthenticated requests get 401; `SageMakerEventBridgeStatus` reads "enabled successfully". The 30-minute watch saw 0 Lambda errors and throttles, and no KMS, SNS, SQS or rescoped-role denial. A temporary Viewer user's single read-only GET returned 200 and was torn down, with deletion proven. Not run (owner or device phase, listed in Resume Here): the alert-delivery check, one auto-labeling job, the redrive check, Quick Setup of a new station and an SSH tunnel. Records: `deploy/deploy-a-result.md`, `deploy/verify-a-result.md`

### 3. Deploy B: EdgeCVPortalComputeStack with R14 DynamoDB

- Preconditions: Deploy A is deployed and its checks passed in this account; section 1's execution-role check allows `kms:CreateGrant` and `kms:DescribeKey`
- Commit Deploy B, once, before the first account's Deploy B, on top of `<deploy-a>`. Check both hash lists first, as in section 2: `cd "$REPO" && sha256sum -c --quiet "$WS/deploy-b-sha256.txt"` passes, and `sha256sum -c --quiet "$WS/deploy-a-snapshot/sha256.txt"` there reports only paths listed in `$WS/deploy-b-paths.txt`; otherwise stop, because a file changed after its hash was recorded. Then `cd "$REPO" && git add --pathspec-from-file="$WS/deploy-b-paths.txt" && git commit -m "fix(security): Deploy B, R14 customer managed key for the account tables"`, written `<deploy-b>`. Afterwards `git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain` lists none of those five paths
- How to read A3 in the commit (task 8 review, finding 2): A3's `reviewed` text in `iam_post_fix_approved_additions.json` says "no wildcard action". Read it as no service-wide or administrative wildcard action. A1's statement grants `kms:GenerateDataKey*` and `kms:ReEncrypt*`, the prefix patterns `grantReadWriteData` emits for a table on a customer managed key, which cover only the data-key and re-encrypt operations. The owner saw that action list in 8.9, so the file stays as approved: rewording it would repeat 8.9 and re-record the file's `deploy-b-sha256.txt` line
- The same deploy steps as section 2, from a worktree at `<deploy-b>` (`git -C "$REPO" worktree add --detach "$WS/deploy-b" <deploy-b>`), removed afterwards. CloudFormation creates the key, then `AccountTablesKeyAccess`, then switches both tables, then updates the default policies
- Review of `npx cdk diff EdgeCVPortalComputeStack <context>`: the only new resources are the key, its alias and `AccountTablesKeyAccess`; the only IAM changes are the key grants, judged per role at grant level as in section 2; nothing is replaced or destroyed; Lambda changes are to code and environment only. Against Deploy A, the key grants appear in `AccountTablesKeyAccess` for all three roles and, from `grantReadWriteData`, in `AccountSyncRoleOverflowPolicy1760E5CF1` and `UserAdminRoleOverflowPolicy190F26973`; no statement moves between carriers, and both tables change only `SSESpecification` and their new dependency on `AccountTablesKeyAccess` (task 8 Result)
- After (design `R14 DynamoDB tests`): `aws dynamodb describe-table --table-name dda-portal-edge-credentials --query Table.SSEDescription` and the same for `dda-portal-account-sync` show `Status` `ENABLED`, `SSEType` `KMS` and the new key's ARN; then list users in the admin console, run an account sync to a test device and remove a test device, with no warnings. Nobody disables the key or schedules its deletion while either table uses it
- Rollback (design `Rollback`): revert and redeploy; both tables switch back to the AWS owned key in place. The key is retained and keeps billing until no backup or recovery point from the key period remains (the point-in-time recovery window, up to 35 days, and any on-demand backup); only then may the owner schedule its deletion
- Result (2026-10-06): committed, pushed, deployed and verified. `<deploy-b>` = `aba57cb` on `remediation` (local, 5 files). Its cherry-pick `3e49de6` (parent `d7bf0d6`, same 5 blobs) passed the IAM synth gate (17/17) and Jest (305/305). The diff changed ComputeStack only, with exactly the key, its alias, `AccountTablesKeyAccess`, both tables' `SSESpecification` and `DependsOn`, and the key grants for the three roles. `3e49de6` was pushed to `origin/integration/all-specs` as a fast-forward at 06:21Z and deployed the same way as Deploy A, 06:22 to 06:28Z, with no failure or rollback. After: both tables are `ENABLED`, `KMS`, on the new key (`alias/dda-portal/account-tables`, rotation on, retained). A temporary PortalAdmin's admin user list, edge-sync device list and device list all returned 200, and the user was torn down with deletion proven. The 30-minute watch saw 0 errors and throttles on the three Lambdas and 0 KMS denials, and every principal's call on the key succeeded. Not run (owner or device phase, listed in Resume Here): an account sync to a test device, removing a test device, and creating a test account or setting a password. Record: `deploy/deploy-b-result.md`

### 4. DDAPortalUseCaseAccountStack (R15 site 4), in each use-case account

- Preconditions: credentials for that use-case account; the portal account id; the account's existing External ID, which `edge-cv-portal/deploy-account-role.sh` offers to reuse from its saved config (reuse it, or the portal's registration of the account stops working); a worktree at `<deploy-b>`, which carries site 4 from Deploy A, created as in section 3 with `npm ci` in its `edge-cv-portal/infrastructure`, and removed afterwards with `git -C "$REPO" worktree remove "$WS/deploy-b"`. It has no ordering constraint against the portal deploys
- Review: `cd "$WS/deploy-b/edge-cv-portal/infrastructure" && npx cdk diff DDAPortalUseCaseAccountStack -a "npx ts-node bin/usecase-account-app.ts" -c portalAccountId=<portal account id> -c externalId=<existing External ID>`. Only `DDASageMakerExecutionRoleDefaultPolicyAABC5DB6` changes, with site 4's four statements, and nothing is replaced or destroyed
- Deploy: `cd "$WS/deploy-b/edge-cv-portal" && ./deploy-account-role.sh`, choosing the use-case account role and reusing the existing External ID when asked. It runs `npx cdk deploy DDAPortalUseCaseAccountStack -a "npx ts-node bin/usecase-account-app.ts" -c portalAccountId=<portal account id> -c externalId=<External ID> --require-approval never`
- After: one training job, one compilation job and one labeling job, each with its logs under `/aws/sagemaker/`. Rollback: redeploy the previous version in that account
- Result (2026-10-06): NOT APPLICABLE. The portal runs in single-account mode: SSM `/dda-portal/trusted-usecase-account-ids` lists only the portal account. `DDAPortalUseCaseAccountStack` is absent in all 18 enabled regions, and it was NOT created. In this mode `deploy-account-role.sh` creates `DDASageMakerExecutionRole` with the AWS CLI, outside any stack, so site 4 doesn't reach it (design `R15 observations`). Record: `deploy/preflight.md` section 10

The pre-swap copy is `$WS/requirements-label-swap/requirements.md.pre-swap`. The original step, kept for the record: the owner's label swap in `requirements.md` (design `Open decisions for the owner`, item 3; task 14.5): review `$WS/requirements-label-swap/requirements.diff`, run `python3 "$WS/label_swap.py" "$SPEC/requirements.md"`, confirm that `sha256sum "$SPEC/requirements.md"` prints the hash 14.5 reported, and re-record it as `REQUIREMENTS_SHA256` in check B of `$WS/check_ledger.py` and `$WS/check_implementation.py`. Then `python3 "$WS/check_requirements.py"` prints `RESULT: PASS`, `python3 "$WS/check_implementation.py"` passes, and `python3 "$WS/label_swap.py" --check "$SPEC"/* "$SPEC"/.config.kiro` exits 0
- Commit, staging each path by name, never with `git add .`: `cd "$REPO"`, then `git add -- <each uncommitted path outside the spec directory that check_implementation.py lists>` and `git commit -m "fix(security): remediate HIGH scan findings in LocalServer and Portal code" -m "Verified on device: <architecture, device and checks for each>"`; then `git add -- .kiro/specs/security-scan-remediation-high` and `git commit -m "docs(spec): add the security-scan-remediation-high spec, ledger and rescan record"`
- Before any push: 14.2's four checks run again and exit 0 over the new commits, and `python3 "$WS/check_implementation.py"` passes
- Push, only when the owner decides under Requirement 18.3, which keeps the branch off the public remote while this spec describes unremediated vulnerabilities: `git -C "$REPO" push -u origin remediation`, to a new remote branch, never to main or master and never forced. Opening a pull request is a separate owner step

### 9. Platform rescan

- Preconditions: section 7's commits exist, and the scanning platform can read the branch at that commit; how it reaches the branch is the owner's decision under Requirement 18.3
- Run the platform scan on the branch at that commit, and export its HIGH findings to a file outside the repository, as for the Scan_Export (Requirement 18.2): `$WS/rescan-platform-<scan id>.json`
- Match: `python3 "$WS/rescan_match.py" --platform-export "$WS/rescan-platform-<scan id>.json"`. The matcher reads `label-map.json`, so the export's original names pair with the relabeled ledger entries
- Record: add the run to `rescan.json` under the platform scan's id, with the platform's `finding_id`s and an entry for every row-2 and row-4 result, as 12.8 does; then `python3 "$WS/label_swap.py" "$SPEC/rescan.json"` (14.3) and `python3 "$WS/check_implementation.py"`
- Expected: the `scanner-x/sns-topic-encryption` entry `bcba7f05-bd61-4f0d-a0ac-672b1d990f2c-0` clears, which only this scan can confirm; this scan decides closure for `avoid-dill`, the rule whose local calibration differed (12.4); totals land near design `Expected rescan results`, 424, or 425 if `feb1c815-fb14-44af-b148-6502a6f1fad8-1` stayed, but the matcher's rows decide
- Close: the scan closes when every result is in row 1, 2 or 4 with a recorded disposition and none is in row 3 (Property 11, Requirements 17.5 and 17.6). A row-3 result reopens its task
- Then: `git -C "$REPO" add -- .kiro/specs/security-scan-remediation-high/rescan.json && git -C "$REPO" commit -m "docs(spec): record the platform rescan"`, pushed under the same decision as section 7
